ash: Migrate LogoutButtonTray colors to ui::ColorId

This CL simplifies `LogoutButtonTray` by setting its button background
and text colors once at construction. Previously, this view was required
to manually handle theme updates by overriding `OnThemeChanged()`.

Following crrev.com/c/6263269, we can pass the `ui::ColorId` directly to
`views::LabelButton::SetEnabledTextColors()`, alongside setting
`views::MdTextButton::SetBgColorIdOverride()` in the constructor. The
button now automatically handles theme changes, eliminating the need for
manual color management.

CONV=d2dcde16-dfba-4397-aa8a-44c55aed547d

cros: Block downloads from captive portal dialogs

ChromeOS captive-portal dialogs built on views::WebDialogView (such as
the "Captive Portal Authorization" dialog on the OOBE/login screen,
in kiosk sessions, and the lock screen) render untrusted web content
controlled by the local network. Previously, these dialogs did not
override WebContentsDelegate::CanDownload, falling back to a permissive
default that allowed a network attacker to silently drop files onto the
device without user interaction or consent UI.

This CL fixes the vulnerability by:
1. Adding `CanDialogDownload()` and `set_can_download()` to
   `ui::WebDialogDelegate` so specific dialogs can opt out of downloads.
2. Overriding `WebContentsDelegate::CanDownload` in `views::WebDialogView`
   to respect the delegate's preference instead of defaulting to true.
3. Explicitly disabling downloads (`set_can_download(false)`) in both
   `SigninWebDialogDelegate` and `LockScreenCaptivePortalDialog`.
4. Adding comprehensive unit and browser tests to prevent regressions.

Bug: b:563014435
Test: testing/xvfb.py out/cros/unit_tests --gtest_filter=WebDialogViewUnitTest.CanDownload && testing/xvfb.py out/cros/browser_tests --gtest_filter='NetworkPortalSigninController*'

[Extensions] Split windows.* browser tests out of tabs_test.cc

Following the split of windows_api.{h,cc} from tabs_api.{h,cc}, moves
the chrome.windows browser tests from tabs_test.cc into a new
windows_test.cc. Tests on the shared ExtensionTabsTest fixture move to
a new ExtensionWindowsTest fixture; ExtensionWindowCreateTest moves
as-is.

While moving ExtensionWindowCreateTest.AcceptState, drop its trailing
content::RunAllPendingInMessageLoop() (a banned pattern). It only
existed to let the Mac-only ScopedFakeNSWindowFullscreen finish its
transition, which was removed when the test was disabled on Mac in
crrev.com/c/7900052. Otherwise there are no test logic changes.

ExtensionWindowCreateIwaTest stays in tabs_test.cc for now since it
shares ExtensionIwaTestBase with the tabs IWA tests.

Bug: 358567092

parent
dd1453c4a27b7d0ec30436d5f788092a540d000a [ diff ] 
[Extensions] Replace tabs_internal with tabs_util for tabs/windows APIs

After splitting windows_api.{h,cc} out of tabs_api.{h,cc}, the two files
still included each other: windows_api.cc used tabs_internal helpers
from tabs_api.h, and tabs_api.cc used from windows_api.h.
This removes that mutual dependency:
- Helpers used by both APIs (GetTabById(), MoveTabToWindow() and
  WindowsCreateFunction::ValidateTab(), now ValidateTabForMove()) move
  to a new tabs_util.{h,cc}.
- Helpers used only by chrome.windows (ApiParameterExtractor,
  kMissingLockWindowFullscreenPrivatePermission,
  ExtensionHasLockedFullscreenPermission(), ConvertToWindowShowState()
  and WindowBoundsIntersectDisplays()) move into windows_api.cc's
  anonymous namespace.
- The remaining helpers are only used by chrome.tabs and move into
  tabs_api.cc's anonymous namespace, so tabs_internal goes away
  (resolving its TODO).

Includes that tabs_api.h and windows_api.h only needed for these
declarations are dropped. No behavior change.

mojo: Trim Broker to shared memory allocation only

After the removal of legacy Mojo Core, Broker and BrokerHost are only
used by BaseSharedMemoryService, which lets sandboxed processes on Linux
and ChromeOS allocate shared memory through the browser process.

Sandboxed processes can't create writable shared memory regions
themselves, because base needs filesystem access to open their read-only
descriptor. So each invitation also connects a Broker in the child
process to a BrokerHost on the browser's IO thread, over a dedicated
socket. Sandboxed content processes install base::SharedMemoryHooks that
call into the Broker, which sends a BUFFER_REQUEST and blocks until the
BrokerHost replies with a BUFFER_RESPONSE carrying the new region's
writable and read-only descriptors.

The rest of the Broker and BrokerHost code was only used by legacy Mojo
Core and has no callers left:

* Delete broker_win.cc and the Windows-only paths (InitData, named
  channels, handle duplication into the client process). The shared
  memory service is not used on Windows.
* Remove BrokerHost::SendChannel() and SendNamedChannel(), and
  BrokerHost's client process and ProcessErrorCallback constructor
  parameters, which were always empty.
* Delete mojo/core/embedder/process_error_callback.h, which was only
  used by BrokerHost.
* Remove Broker's wait_for_channel_handle mode and GetInviterEndpoint().
* Build the broker sources only where SHARED_MEMORY_SERVICE_REQUIRED()
  holds, rather than building BrokerHost on all platforms.

The wire format of BUFFER_REQUEST and BUFFER_RESPONSE is unchanged.

Revert "[glic] Start benchmark: remove harness artifacts from the cold number"

This reverts commit b0abae75dc17492cfe174713d8b92301277c80ed.

Reason for revert:
LUCI Bisection has identified this change as the cause of a test failure. See the analysis: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/bisection/test-analysis/b/4913988950294528

Sample build with failed test: https://ci.chromium.org/b/8668371221168209297
Affected test(s):
[://chrome/test\:android_browsertests!gtest::GlicInitializationBenchmark#MeasureInitializationTime/All.NoWebview](https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/:%2F%2Fchrome%2Ftest%5C:android_browsertests%21gtest::GlicInitializationBenchmark%23MeasureInitializationTime%2FAll.NoWebview?q=VHash%3Aa9f588fc2a06c8ec)
[://chrome/test\:android_browsertests!gtest::GlicInitializationBenchmark#MeasureInitializationTime/All.Webview](https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/:%2F%2Fchrome%2Ftest%5C:android_browsertests%21gtest::GlicInitializationBenchmark%23MeasureInitializationTime%2FAll.Webview?q=VHash%3Aa9f588fc2a06c8ec)

If this is a false positive, please report it at http://b.corp.google.com/createIssue?component=1199205&description=Analysis%3A+https%3A%2F%2Fci.chromium.org%2Fui%2Fp%2Fchromium%2Fbisection%2Ftest-analysis%2Fb%2F4913988950294528&format=PLAIN&priority=P3&title=Wrongly+blamed+https%3A%2F%2Fchromium-review.googlesource.com%2Fc%2Fchromium%2Fsrc%2F%2B%2F8546772&type=BUG

Original change's description:
> [glic] Start benchmark: remove harness artifacts from the cold number
>
> The default HTTP/1 EmbeddedTestServer closes the connection after every
> response, so each guest script fetch paid a TCP+TLS handshake;
> production serves the client over one HTTP/2 connection. The test client
> also imported WebClientMode as a value, pulling the 46 KB generated API
> into a three-deep import waterfall that production never fetches. And a
> fresh test browser paid network-service initialization on the first
> open.
>
> Serve the guest from an H2 server (new
> GlicBrowserTestMixin::set_glic_https_test_server() hook; other fixtures
> are unchanged), make the client's API imports type-only with
> modulepreload for the remaining two files, and warm the network service
> with one navigation to a separate origin before the timer. The guest
> origin is left cold on purpose: Chrome does not preconnect for Glic, so
> its DNS/TCP/TLS is real cold-open cost. Also wait for a ready spare
> renderer before timing, log nav/resource timing per iteration, and have
> run_glic_start_benchmark.py report cold and warm separately.
>
> Bug: b:572041114

parent
289f9ba0e59cf28ce37ee1e995b0ab85e4a0424e [ diff ] 
[Blink] Clear stale shaping when line breaking overflows

BreakText() can retry an existing InlineItemResult at a smaller width
when HandleOverflow() attempts to fit part of a text item. When
ShapeLine() returns no result (kNoResultIfOverflow), the overflow path
extends the text range to the end of the item but leaves shaping from
the previous attempt attached, so the InlineItemResult fails
CheckConsistency() before HandleOverflow() restores its saved result.

Clear the stale shape on this path, restoring the invariant that
shape_result is null after a no-result overflow. HandleOverflow() always
restores its saved result afterwards, so the failure is DCHECK-only and
release layout behavior is unchanged.

Guard the fix with the stable LineBreakerClearShapeResultOnOverflow
runtime feature so it can be disabled as a kill switch.

Add a WPT crash test. Fractional letter-spacing advances make the
shaped width of a fitting break opportunity land one LayoutUnit above
the available width, so the HandleOverflow() retry at a smaller width
returns no result and the stale shape trips CheckConsistency(). The
test passes with the feature enabled and hits the DCHECK when the
feature is disabled.

Test: run_web_tests.py external/wpt/css/css-text/crashtests/overflow-wrap-anywhere-fractional-width-crash.html
Fixed: 559557312

parent
0dcb3c5184450682c75eac5d98acb18daedb243f [ diff ] 
sensors: Remove DanglingUntriaged from PlatformSensorReaderWinrt

PlatformSensorReaderWinrtBase::client_ was annotated DanglingUntriaged
because the unit tests freed the mock client while the reader still
pointed to it. Readers returned by CreateAndInitializeSensor() were
owned through base::OnTaskRunnerDeleter, so their deletion was posted
to the COM STA task runner and ran after the test body had already
destroyed the mock client.

Production code is not affected: ~PlatformSensorWin() calls
SetClient(nullptr) before the client goes away. Make the tests follow
the same contract: CreateAndInitializeSensor() now returns the reader
with a deleter that calls SetClient(nullptr) on the main thread before
posting deletion to the COM STA task runner, and the mock client moves
into the test fixture so that it outlives the reader. Remove the
DanglingUntriaged annotation and update the SetClient() comment to
describe the lifetime contract.

Tested on Windows with is_component_build=false and
enable_dangling_raw_ptr_checks=true, running services_unittests with
the PartitionAllocBackupRefPtr and PartitionAllocDanglingPtr features:
- Annotation removed, tests unchanged: the dangling pointer detector
  fires in PlatformSensorReaderTestWinrt (3 crashed, 20 not run).
- With this change: all 27 PlatformSensorReaderTestWinrt tests and
  all 116 tests matching *Sensor* pass.

Fixed: 328023340

[2/N]: WebMCP: Identify tools by a unique ID for unregistration

This CL gives every WebMCP tool a unique, unguessable, non-web-exposed
`tool_id`, minted by the registering renderer and carried on
`blink.mojom.ScriptTool`. The browser verifies that the ID is present
and unique within a document upon registration, and tools are now
unregistered by ID rather than by name, through a single
`ModelContext::UnregisterTool(tool_id)` entry point:
 - An imperative tool's unregister-on-abort algorithm is bound to the
   ID minted for that tool.
 - A declarative tool's backing `<form>` tool object owns its ID, just
   as it owns its name, and unregisters with it.

This fixes a race where unregistering one tool could remove a
*different* tool registered under the same name. Today this is only
reachable through declarative tools: a `<form>` whose registration was
rejected because its name was already taken (by an imperative or
declarative tool) would, upon removal or renaming, unregister by name
and take out the unrelated tool that owned the name. New WPTs cover
this, and fail without this CL.

The imperative abort path cannot currently hit this race from script:
an imperative tool can only be unregistered by its own signal, and a
rejected imperative registration never attaches an abort algorithm.
It is moved to IDs for consistency, and to stay correct if another
unregistration path is ever added.

This CL does NOT fix the analogous execution-time race: `executeTool()`
still targets tools by name, so a stale `RegisteredTool` dictionary can
execute a different tool that was since registered under the same
name. Fixing that requires the forthcoming `Tool` interface, which
carries the tool's ID and executes by it.

`tool_id` is optional in mojom for now, since `ScriptTool` is also used
to describe tools to embedders (APC, the actor), which do not populate
it yet. Follow-ups will plumb IDs through APC and CDP, and then make
`tool_id` required.

See
https://docs.google.com/document/d/1psoB7k71-Dgkxg2tTG62DYJDTg02x8zOS3jCEd0QlbM/edit?tab=t.0.

R=mfoltz

tools/licenses: Remove obsolete and stale SPECIAL_CASES entries

Remove obsolete SPECIAL_CASES entries:

- third_party/angle: Already has correct metadata
  (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/angle/README.chromium).

- third_party/crashpad/crashpad/third_party/getopt: Already has correct
  metadata
  (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/crashpad/crashpad/third_party/getopt/README.crashpad).

- third_party/crashpad/crashpad/third_party/xnu: Already has correct
  metadata
  (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/crashpad/crashpad/third_party/xnu/README.crashpad).

- third_party/dawn/third_party/khronos: Migrated to standard third_party
  layout (third_party/EGL-Registry/src) with README.chromium in
  https://dawn-review.googlesource.com/c/dawn/+/298335
  (crbug.com/493763028).

- native_client: Removed from checkout in https://crrev.com/c/6632728
  (crbug.com/423859723). Also remove stale native_client entry from
  ADDITIONAL_PATHS.

- third_party/openscreen/src/third_party/mozilla: Removed from
  OpenScreen in https://crrev.com/c/4933405.

- third_party/ppapi, third_party/v8-i18n: Obsolete directories no
  longer in DEPS or tree. Also remove from KNOWN_NON_IOS_LIBRARIES.

Note: third_party/ipcz is retained in SPECIAL_CASES because its local
README.chromium sets URL to prose text rather than a valid hyperlink.

Bug: 493763028, 423859723

parent
b7db82e9700cc58427c4259398b3fde66fe31193 [ diff ] 
[net] Remove the expired ReportingAndNEL.NumberOfLoaded* histograms

SQLitePersistentReportingAndNelStore recorded how many NEL policies,
Reporting endpoints and endpoint groups it loaded, under four
histograms that carry a TODO to remove them once the investigation in
crbug.com/40054414 was done. All four have expired
(ReportingAndNEL.NumberOfLoadedNELPolicies{,2} on 2023-06-25 and
ReportingAndNEL.NumberOfLoadedReportingEndpoint{s,Groups}2 on
2024-09-15), so the code that records them is dead.

Remove the histogram names, the three Record*() helpers and their call
sites, keeping the DCHECKs that an unsuccessful load returns nothing,
and delete the histograms.xml entries.

R=ricea@chromium.org

Bug: 40054414

1da364ed4d20310d93a0fbec176e756e1fe4af08

aab615805d04e3da3f8829d049f39304c8cfd4bf [ diff ] 
ksadmin: Optimize switch alias lookups using FixedFlatMap

Replace the static base::NoDestructor map in HasSwitch() with a
constexpr base::FixedFlatMap storing spans of string_view to avoid heap
allocation and static initialization overhead. Additionally, avoid
redundant map lookups in SwitchValue() and HasSwitch() by retaining
iterators returned by find().

Bug: 452950815

6d90771a0ae7004c08a88efcd21dfe51ec6384ff [ diff ] 
Restore mouse cursor for native dialogs on Windows

When the mouse cursor is hidden while typing, native dialogs can inherit
a hidden cursor because: 1. Calling ::ShowCursor(FALSE) in
DesktopWindowTreeHostWin decrements the
   Win32 thread-global cursor counter below 0.
2. Mouse events over native dialogs bypass Chrome's event filter, so
   Chrome never unhides the cursor.

This CL fixes this by: 1. Stopping ::ShowCursor() calls in
DesktopWindowTreeHostWin (per-window
   visibility is already handled via CursorType::kNone).
   This is most likely legacy code that is no longer necessary.
2. Restoring cursor visibility in HandleCancelMode() when a modal dialog
   opens, so the cursor is restored immediately without mouse movement
   and CursorClient stays in sync.
3. Guarding these changes behind features::kHideCursorWhileTyping, so
  that if this ever causes a regression, we can disable it to diagnose
  the issue.

Bug: 567626032

[NTP Customization Sync] Normalize NTP background URL options in theme translation

When NtpBackgroundService constructs theme collection image URLs from
the Backdrop service, it appends platform-specific FIFE image options
via GetImageOptions():
- Android & iOS: =s2556-k-no-nd
- Desktop: =w3840-h2160-p-k-no-nd-mv

Previously, TranslateDesktop, TranslateIos, and TranslateAndroid in
theme_translation.cc copied ntp_background() verbatim without replacing
the source platform's URL options suffix with the local platform's
GetImageOptions(). Because downstream consumers compare full URL strings
directly, the mismatched suffix caused cross-platform theme equality
checks, theme history deduplication, active theme collection selection
highlighting, and Backdrop URL validation to fail between Desktop and
Mobile.

This CL:
1. Adds NormalizeNtpBackgroundUrl() in theme_translation.cc to strip any
   existing image options via RemoveOptionsFromImageURL() and append the
   local platform's GetImageOptions() via AddOptionsToImageURL() across
   TranslateDesktop, TranslateIos, and TranslateAndroid. Only valid
   HTTP(S) URLs of theme collection backgrounds (non-empty
   collection_id) are normalized; everything else is left unchanged.
2. Moves the image URL option helpers (GetImageOptions(),
   GetThumbnailImageOptions(), AddOptionsToImageURL(),
   RemoveOptionsFromImageURL()) from components/themes/ntp_background_data
   into a new foundational //components/themes/common target, so that
   //components/themes/cross_device can depend on them without a
   child -> parent directory dependency. ntp_background_data.h still
   includes the new header, so existing callers are unaffected.
3. Adds unit tests in cross_device_theme_tracker_unittest.cc verifying
   cross-platform URL normalization and theme equality, and that
   empty/unset, non-HTTP(S), invalid, and non-collection URLs are left
   unchanged.

Analysis: https://docs.google.com/document/d/16YTVkec9bWAR6GHjRRVL9Ei62uajiTwiMPLaO_DagJs/edit?usp=sharing&resourcekey=0-coKKPibv-f4TQBINdCw3_g

TAG=agy
CONV=9a0411f4-0500-48f3-8472-93db77df2b0c
